# Docket — Launch Compliance Scorecard

> **Status**: Audit complete — 399 of 399 requirements adjudicated against observed evidence.
> **Last Updated**: 2026-08-02
> **Machine-readable source of truth**: [`launch-compliance.json`](./launch-compliance.json)

Every requirement below was mechanically derived from the author's production-launch plan — the plan text was decomposed clause by clause, so each row traces back to something the author actually wrote rather than to an interpretation of what he might have meant. Each requirement carries the author's own words in its `sourceQuote` field in the JSON, alongside the `acceptance` criteria that decide whether it is met.

**Every status here is backed by observed evidence, not by an agent's self-report.** The `evidence` column records what was actually run, read, measured, or looked at — a command and its output, a file and line, a live HTTP response, a screenshot that was opened and inspected. Where a verdict rests on something weaker than the acceptance criteria demand, the JSON's `notes` field says so explicitly. No requirement was marked complete because an implementer said it was.

**Statuses mean exactly this:**

| Status         | Meaning                                                                                          |
| -------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `pass`         | The acceptance criteria were met and the evidence demonstrates it.                               |
| `partial`      | Real work exists and some clauses hold, but at least one acceptance clause is unmet or unproven. |
| `fail`         | The capability exists in some form and demonstrably does not meet the requirement.               |
| `not-built`    | The capability, artifact, or test the requirement names does not exist at all.                   |
| `unverifiable` | **Could not be determined either way in this environment.**                                      |

`unverifiable` means exactly that and **must never be read as `pass`**. It marks requirements where the evidence needed to decide was out of reach — a production instance that could not be signed into, a fixture that does not exist, a surface with no data to render, a tool the acceptance is defined against that has not been built. Each of those 9 rows still needs to be settled before launch; none of them is a quiet approval.

---

## Summary

| Metric                          |   Count |
| ------------------------------- | ------: |
| Total requirements              |     399 |
| Audited                         |     399 |
| Pass                            |      19 |
| Partial                         |      96 |
| Fail                            |     103 |
| Not built                       |     172 |
| Unverifiable                    |       9 |
| **Launch blockers outstanding** | **130** |

Severity split across all 399 requirements: **138 launch-blocker**, 221 high, 40 medium. Of the 138 launch-blockers, 130 are outstanding (anything not `pass`) and 8 pass.

The requirement demands grab → grabbing → grab with no sampled frame reporting 'pointer'.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `INT-22`   | Accessibility                              | Every interactive element on every app surface is reachable and operable using the keyboard alone.                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | `fail`         | No keyboard-only E2E suite exists: `grep -rl "press('Tab')" apps/web/e2e apps/web/tests` returns only apps/web/e2e/athena-personal.spec.ts and apps/web/e2e/helpers/calendar-ui.ts (feature specs, not an accessibility sweep); there is no a11y spec file. My own Tab sweep over 10 core surfaces (scratchpad/audit.ts → scratchpad/out/results.json, a11y.\*.keyboard) reached fewer elements than the visible focusable-candidate count on 6 of 10 surfaces: inbox 34/39, myWork 35/36, initiativeDetail 49/51, projectDetail 49/53, taskDetail 55/57, athena 41/42, settings 44/46 (today 43/43, portfolio 59/59, initiatives 38/38 were clean). Zero focus drops to <body> were observed on any surface.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `MISS-02`  | Core Functionality / Data integrity        | Every kind of data Docket stores — not only dates — must be protected by strong constraints at the schema and API boundary.                                                                                                                                                                                         | `fail`         | The required schema-audit test does not exist: no test enumerates Drizzle tables to assert enum/CHECK, NOT NULL FK + ON DELETE, uniqueness or range invariants. packages/db/tests/schema/ contains athena-schema, db, execution-nonce, initiative-experience-schema, notification-service-schema, personal-athena-schema and search specs; db.test.ts asserts FK resolvability and $defaultFn/$onUpdate behaviour (15 tests pass), not constraint policy. Several invariants the acceptance names have no table at all — published-domain host and action id do not exist (CORE-30, INT-32). Concrete constraint gaps found by inspection: packages/db/src/schema/work.ts declares task `state: text('state').notNull()` — an enumerated workflow-state domain stored as free text with no pg enum and no CHECK, while sibling columns correctly use pg enums (priority: taskPriority, status: projectStatus, health, visibility); packages/db/src/schema/agents.ts:198-199 declares agentSessionDispatch `action: text('action').$type<AgentSessionDispatchAction>()` and `status: text('status').$type<AgentSessionDispatchStatus>()` — closed unions enforced only in TypeScript, not in the database; packages/db/src/schema/agents.ts:83 agentSession.taskId is a nullable FK for what ATH-35/37 treat as a required relationship. POSITIVE, verified: the API boundary does reject out-of-domain values with a 4xx Problem rather than coercing — `PATCH /v1/orgs/{orgId}/tasks/{id} {"startDate":"2026-09-15T00:00:00.000Z"}` returned HTTP 422 `{"type":".../problems/validation_error","code":"validation_error","fieldErrors":{"startDate":[{"code":"invalid_format","format":"date"}]}}`. Real constraints do exist widely (pg enums, CASCADE/SET NULL FKs, unique indexes such as organization_slug_uq, CHECKs such as task_dependency_no_self and initiative_hierarchy_no_self). |

## Findings by area

One section per area, in the order the areas appear in the plan. Each table is `id | requirement | severity | status | evidence`; the author's source quote, the full acceptance criteria, and the auditor's caveats for every row live in [`launch-compliance.json`](./launch-compliance.json).

### Security

2 requirements — 2 partial.

| ID       | Requirement                                                                                                  | Severity | Status    | Evidence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |

| `GEN-06` | The delivered system must minimize security holes introduced during the launch push.                         | high     | `partial` | No secret scanner exists: `grep -rn 'gitleaks\|trufflehog' .github scripts package.json` → no matches; `.github/workflows/ci.yml` has only quality/test/build/e2e/deploy jobs; `which gitleaks trufflehog` → not found. Manual regex scan over all tracked files (git ls-files \| xargs grep -E 'sk-ant-\|sk*live*\|AKIA\|ghp\_\|BEGIN PRIVATE KEY\|xoxb-\|AIza') → zero hits. `.env.local` IS tracked (git ls-files) but git show HEAD:.env.local contains only empty/placeholder values plus a self-labelled dev-only BETTER_AUTH_SECRET. ### Chips & pills

| ID         | Requirement                                                                                                                                                             | Severity       | Status      | Evidence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| ---------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------- | ----------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `CRAFT-11` | Pill/chip structures render from one shared component so they are visually consistent, specifically in the new-object (task, initiative) dialogs the author called out. | launch-blocker | `fail`      | Measured live via Playwright getBoundingClientRect/getComputedStyle in both dialogs (script + raw data: scratchpad/audit.ts, scratchpad/out/results.json). NEW-INITIATIVE dialog — 9 pills, 3 distinct heights [28, 32, 36]px, 2 distinct paddings ['12px/12px','8px/8px'], icon sizes 24x24 or absent. NEW-TASK dialog — 7 pills, 3 distinct heights [20, 28, 36]px, paddings ['0px/0px','8px/8px'], icon sizes 16x16 vs 24x24 vs absent. Requirement demands 0px height variance; actual variance is 8px (initiative) and 16px (task). Screenshots looked at: scratchpad/out/new-initiative-dialog-1440-light.png shows three visually distinct pill families on adjacent rows (template pills 'Blank'/'Strategic initiative'/'Objective' are taller and bolder; property pills '+ Set owner'/'+ Set target'/'+ Set health' carry 24px plus-icons; value pills 'Active'/'No priority'/'Monthly updates' are shortest and icon-less). scratchpad/out/new-task-dialog-1440-light.png shows the same split ('Backlog' 16px icon vs '+ Set project' 24px icon vs bare 'No priority'). |
| `CRAFT-12` | Deep research into how MD3 specifies chip/pill structures is performed and written down as a referenceable document.                                                    | high           | `not-built` | `grep -rn "m3.material.io" docs/` returns ZERO hits repo-wide. `grep -rn -i "assist chip\|filter chip\|input chip\|suggestion chip" docs/` returns only product-feature prose about Initiatives acting as timeline filter chips (docs/core/mvp-plan.md:246,261,265; docs/engineering/specs/design-system.md:543; docs/engineering/specs/api-rpc-contract.md:381) — not MD3 spec citations. docs/ contains no research/ directory; docs/design/ holds only audits/, craft-rubric.md, ghost-grammar.md. design-system.md mentions chips only as component names in an inventory (lines 302, 439, 520) with no spec section references, no size/state tables, and no mapping of Docket pill usage to a named MD3 component + token set.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `CRAFT-13` | Pills without icons are eliminated — a pill displaying only a bare text label must not ship.                                                                            | high           | `fail`      | Measured + looked at. NEW-INITIATIVE dialog has 6 icon-less pills: 'Blank', 'Strategic initiative', 'Objective', 'Active', 'No priority', 'Monthly updates' (scratchpad/out/results.json → initiativeDialog.pills, hasIcon:false; visible in scratchpad/out/new-initiative-dialog-1440-light.png). NEW-TASK dialog has 1 bare-text pill: 'No priority' ('LE' is an avatar glyph, which the acceptance permits) — scratchpad/out/new-task-dialog-1440-light.png. INITIATIVE DETAIL property row has 3 icon-less pills: 'Active', 'No priority', 'Monthly' — I cropped and zoomed the row to confirm (source apps/web/.data/design-review/launch-audit/base/orgs-orgId-initiatives-01KZ0S4B2C0FJ2SJKNHKXB08V8-1440x900-light.png, zoom /tmp/pills_zoom.png): 'Active', 'No priority' and 'Monthly' render as plain text in a rounded container while their row-mates '+ No health', '+ Set target date', '+ Set owner', '+ Add labels' carry plus-icons and 'No verdict yet' carries a dot. Count of unjustified icon-less pills is 10 across three surfaces, not zero.               |

Fields bypass it wholesale in apps/web/src (excluding tests): 36 raw `<input` (e.g. sign-in/page.tsx:284, calendar-toolbar.tsx:160, initiatives-client.tsx:608, settings/calendar/page.tsx:214/233/299), 10 raw `<textarea` (settings/athena/page.tsx:97, today-prompt.tsx:145, athena-conversation.tsx:196, athena-workbench.tsx:249, CommentActivityFeed.tsx:263, updates-panel.tsx:146, activity-item.tsx:281), 30 raw `<select`. | `CRAFT-26` | Inputs carry no unnecessary shadow in any state.                                                                                                                          | launch-blocker | `fail` | packages/ui/src/primitives/input.tsx:22 — the primitive's own default class string contains `shadow-sm`: `'border-outline-variant ... flex h-9 w-full rounded-md border bg-transparent px-3 py-1 shadow-sm transition-colors ...'`. This is an unconditional default-state box-shadow, not a focus ring (the focus ring is separately composed as `focusRing` on line 23).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |

Broad probe across 14 routes found 24-53 flex rows per route whose visible siblings differ in height by >0.5px (scratchpad/craft/craft.json inlineHeightViolationCount). |

| `CRAFT-29` | The app is actually visually aligned — repeated rows and property lists share common alignment axes.        | launch-blocker | `unverifiable` | The CRAFT-28 audit this requirement is defined against does not exist (see CRAFT-28), so 'reports zero offenders' cannot be evaluated. My substitute probe (scratchpad/craft/craft.json alignIssues) reported 0 issues on 13 of 14 routes and 2 on /portfolio, but both /portfolio hits are a wrapping chip row (`flex min-w-0 flex-wrap gap-1.5`, 18 children, 426px left spread) — a false positive of my heuristic, not a real misalignment.                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `CRAFT-30` | Padding and margins are sufficient that text and icons never touch or border the edges of their containers. | launch-blocker | `fail`         | No automated inset measurement exists in the repo. My own Tab sweep over 10 core surfaces (scratchpad/audit.ts → scratchpad/out/results.json, a11y.\*.keyboard) reached fewer elements than the visible focusable-candidate count on 6 of 10 surfaces: inbox 34/39, myWork 35/36, initiativeDetail 49/51, projectDetail 49/53, taskDetail 55/57, athena 41/42, settings 44/46 (today 43/43, portfolio 59/59, initiatives 38/38 were clean). No committed copy of the MCP Tasks spec exists (grep for 'modelcontextprotocol.io/extensions/tasks' across docs/ returned nothing), so every method/capability/field name asserted by the tests is hard-coded from memory, which the acceptance explicitly forbids. | `ATH-18` | `tasks/get` must return current status and, in terminal states, the final result or error, with task ids durable across disconnect and restart.                   | launch-blocker | `fail`      | Durability is architecturally absent. apps/api/src/mcp/task-store.ts:11 holds `const sharedStore = new InMemoryTaskStore()` plus a module-level `taskOwners` Map at :12 — process memory only, so no task id can survive a server restart. apps/api/src/mcp/server.ts:40-50 documents that a fresh McpServer is built per request for the stateless Streamable HTTP transport. No lifecycle test exists: the only coverage (mcp-surface.test.ts:413-442) creates a task and immediately reads tasks/result; nothing polls tasks/get through `working` → terminal, nothing asserts `completed` carries result vs `failed` carries error, and nothing tears down and reconnects.                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `ATH-19` | Mid-flight input must be supported: a task moves to `input_required` with `inputRequests`, and the client fulfills them via `tasks/update`.                       | high           | `not-built` | `grep -rn 'tasks/update\|input_required\|inputRequests\|inputResponses' --include='*.ts' --include='*.md' apps packages docs` returned zero matches in any implementation or spec (the only hits repo-wide were unrelated substrings in docs/core/mvp-plan.md:189 and docs/engineering/build-manifest.md:251, both the phrase 'tasks/updates' in prose). apps/api/src/mcp/task-tools.ts implements only createTask/getTask/getTaskResult.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `ATH-20` | `tasks/cancel` must be implemented and acknowledged.                                                                                                              | high           | `partial`   | Implemented and tested: server.ts:66 advertises `cancel: {}`; apps/api/tests/mcp/mcp-surface.test.ts:444-520 sends tasks/cancel on an in-flight task, asserts `cancelled.status === 'cancelled'` and that a subsequent getTaskResult rejects. Gap: cancellation intent is not recorded when the work wins the race — apps/api/src/mcp/task-tools.ts:44-53 catches the storeTaskResult rejection and silently discards it with the comment 'The task may already have been cancelled; cancellation wins the race.' Nothing is persisted. Also gated off by default (MCP_TASKS_ENABLED=false).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `ATH-21` | Task status updates must be pushable via `notifications/tasks` rather than requiring polling.                                                                     | high           | `not-built` | `grep -rn 'notifications/tasks\|subscriptions/listen' --include='*.ts' --include='*.md' apps packages docs` returned zero matches. server.ts:65-67 advertises only `tasks.list`, `tasks.cancel`, and `tasks.requests.tools.call` — no notification or subscription sub-capability. The task capability object contains no push channel at all.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `ATH-22` | MCP tasks must model only work that is in progress or has execution pending — finished or non-executing Docket work must not be exposed as MCP tasks.             | high           | `partial`   | Structurally correct by construction: exactly two tools declare `execution: { taskSupport: 'optional' }` (apps/api/src/mcp/session-tools.ts:120 and view-plan-tools.ts:185); every other tool is 'forbidden' (catalog.ts:159, catalog.ts:328, list-metadata.ts:140). apps/api/src/mcp/task-store.ts only ever receives handles created by createTaskToolHandler (task-tools.ts:30) for an in-flight tool execution — Docket work rows are never inserted into it, so a completed/cancelled/backlog Docket task cannot appear. Missing: no test seeds completed/cancelled/backlog Docket tasks alongside a running job and asserts the MCP listing contains only the running job, and no test asserts terminal-state eviction past ttlMs (DEFAULT_TASK_TTL_MS = 5min at task-tools.ts:10).                                                                                                             |

(1) Agent updates do not flow through it: the only publisher in the codebase is apps/api/src/routes/stream-helpers.ts:100 (`for (const r of recipients) publish(r.userId, toStreamEventOut(row, r.reason))`); `grep -rn "emitEvent" apps/api/src/agent/*.ts apps/api/src/routes/agent-session*.ts` returns nothing, so agent activity is written to sessionActivity only. (2) No replay: event-bus.ts:53-54 `const set = subscribers.get(userId); if (!set) return;` — an event published before a subscriber attaches is dropped and is not readable afterward. |

The canonical example in packages/agent-runtime/src/mock-agent-runtime.ts:23 is exactly the anti-pattern: `{ type: 'elicitation', body: 'Should I also assign this task to you?' }`. The card renders only that text plus a reply box (apps/web/src/components/agents/activity-item.tsx:129-137).                                                          |

The parent session's task link is itself nullable and was null in my live probe: POST /v1/orgs/01KZ0R2DY0MNFG8BX4P297NQ9J/sessions returned `{"id":"01KZ0SHKT0J661TXDBT9QQKD4E","taskId":null,...}`.                                                                                                                                                |

### Core Functionality / Time Tracking

No /time route exists in the route listing (find apps/web/src/app -name page.tsx). Confirmed visually — no timer control on Today (apps/web/.data/design-review/audit-baseline/today-1440x900-light.png), Athena (athena-1440x900-light.png), or task detail (audit-taskdetail/…-1440x900-light.png).                                                                     |

Implementation at apps/api/src/time/commands.ts:325-350 (pauseTimeRecord closes the active interval) and :250-322 (startTimeRecord opens a new one). | `ENT-20` | It must be possible to set a task's anticipated start date. | high     | `partial` | API PASSES: task.startDate is a distinct 'Planned start date' field. Schema packages/db/src/schema/work.ts declares `startDate: timestamp('start_date')` and `dueDate: timestamp('due_date')` as separate columns. Live OpenAPI documents startDate as 'Planned start date (ISO date string)' vs dueDate 'Target due date', and lists startDate in the POST /v1/orgs/{orgId}/tasks body. Verified round-trip against the running API: `PATCH $API_URL/v1/orgs/$A/tasks/$KA {"startDate":"2026-09-15","dueDate":"2026-10-01"}` → `{'startDate': '2026-09-15T00:00:00.000Z', 'dueDate': '2026-10-01T00:00:00.000Z'}`, and an independent re-fetch returned the same values, so it persists and survives a re-read and is distinct from due date. UI FAILS: the task detail exposes no anticipated-start control. apps/web/src/app/(app)/orgs/[orgId]/tasks/[taskId]/page.tsx has exactly one date control, lines 264-270 (`value={task.dueDate ...}` / `placeholder="Set due date"` / `ariaLabel="Due date"`). apps/web/src/components/task-detail/task-properties-rail.tsx renders only Project, Program, Milestone, Cycle, Estimate, Source, Created — no start or due row. `grep -rn "startDate" apps/web/src/components/tasks apps/web/src/components/task-detail apps/web/src/components/property-pickers` returns zero hits, and the new-task dialog shows only a 'Due date' pill (scratchpad/out/new-task-dialog-1440-light.png). |

The live OpenAPI document from the running API (236 paths) contains no enforcement or completion-event endpoint. The other five have no operation at all: no mark-block-started/finished, no report-task-completion Curfew operation, no receive/emit enforcement directives, no day-start or day-end review-state signal. Scanning every path+summary+description for device/lock/enforc/curfew/directive produced only incidental word matches in unrelated CRUD summaries.                                                                                                                                                                   |

But the acceptance's second clause has no subject — there is no dedicated integration adapter module to delete or disable, because the integration does not exist (see WIL-25). The only artifact naming Curfew is the design doc.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `WIL-28` | A clear document or plan exists that explicitly outlines how to implement the Docket/Curfew user experience. | launch-blocker | `partial`   | docs/engineering/specs/curfew-integration.md exists and is substantive — 554 lines with §0 coupling rule, §1 coupling boundary, §2 daily flow, §3 API surface (3.1 auth, 3.2 docket://hub/directive, 3.3 acknowledge_directive, 3.4 plan_day reuse, 3.5 delivery, 3.6 versioning), §4 posture computation, §5 realism, §6 non-goals, §7 net-new checklist. Only one 'placeholder' string appears (line 456) and it is descriptive prose, not a stub. It never says "lock the screen," "quit this app," or "run this command."' Lines 136-139 add: 'There is no third option where Docket sends an imperative enforcement command — that vocabulary does not exist on either side of this integration by design.' The §7 Curfew-side checklist closes with an unchecked item calling the 'literally impossible' enforcement gap 'explicitly out of this integration's scope' that 'contradicts curfew-sync.md's current non-goals'. No enforcement-directive type, no release-condition type, and no integration test exist in the codebase. |

### Core Functionality / Data integrity

1 requirement — 1 fail · 1 launch-blocker outstanding.

| ID        | Requirement                                                                                                                 | Severity       | Status | Evidence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| --------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------- | ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `MISS-02` | Every kind of data Docket stores — not only dates — must be protected by strong constraints at the schema and API boundary. | launch-blocker | `fail` | The required schema-audit test does not exist: no test enumerates Drizzle tables to assert enum/CHECK, NOT NULL FK + ON DELETE, uniqueness or range invariants. packages/db/tests/schema/ contains athena-schema, db, execution-nonce, initiative-experience-schema, notification-service-schema, personal-athena-schema and search specs; db.test.ts asserts FK resolvability and $defaultFn/$onUpdate behaviour (15 tests pass), not constraint policy. Several invariants the acceptance names have no table at all — published-domain host and action id do not exist (CORE-30, INT-32). Concrete constraint gaps found by inspection: packages/db/src/schema/work.ts declares task `state: text('state').notNull()` — an enumerated workflow-state domain stored as free text with no pg enum and no CHECK, while sibling columns correctly use pg enums (priority: taskPriority, status: projectStatus, health, visibility); packages/db/src/schema/agents.ts:198-199 declares agentSessionDispatch `action: text('action').$type<AgentSessionDispatchAction>()` and `status: text('status').$type<AgentSessionDispatchStatus>()` — closed unions enforced only in TypeScript, not in the database; packages/db/src/schema/agents.ts:83 agentSession.taskId is a nullable FK for what ATH-35/37 treat as a required relationship. POSITIVE, verified: the API boundary does reject out-of-domain values with a 4xx Problem rather than coercing — `PATCH /v1/orgs/{orgId}/tasks/{id} {"startDate":"2026-09-15T00:00:00.000Z"}` returned HTTP 422 `{"type":".../problems/validation_error","code":"validation_error","fieldErrors":{"startDate":[{"code":"invalid_format","format":"date"}]}}`. Real constraints do exist widely (pg enums, CASCADE/SET NULL FKs, unique indexes such as organization_slug_uq, CHECKs such as task_dependency_no_self and initiative_hierarchy_no_self). |
